### 128K tokens, batch 1, FP8

KV cache sizing at 131,072 tokens, batch 1

| Model | Org | Architecture | Layers | dtype | Bytes/token (avg) | Total per sequence | Total (batch) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Llama 3 8B † | Meta | GQA | 32 | fp8 | 64.00 KiB | 8.00 GiB | 8.00 GiB |
| Llama 3.1 70B | Meta | GQA | 80 | fp8 | 160.00 KiB | 20.00 GiB | 20.00 GiB |
| Qwen2.5 7B | Alibaba | GQA | 28 | fp8 | 28.00 KiB | 3.50 GiB | 3.50 GiB |
| Qwen3 8B † | Alibaba | GQA | 36 | fp8 | 72.00 KiB | 9.00 GiB | 9.00 GiB |
| DeepSeek-V2 | DeepSeek-AI | MLA | 60 | fp8 | 33.75 KiB | 4.22 GiB | 4.22 GiB |
| DeepSeek-V3 | DeepSeek-AI | MLA | 61 | fp8 | 34.31 KiB | 4.29 GiB | 4.29 GiB |
| Kimi K2 Instruct | Moonshot AI | MLA | 61 | fp8 | 34.31 KiB | 4.29 GiB | 4.29 GiB |
| Mistral 7B v0.1 † | Mistral AI | HYBRID | 32 | fp8 | 2.00 KiB | 256.00 MiB | 256.00 MiB |
| Gemma 2 9B † | Google | HYBRID | 42 | fp8 | 86.62 KiB | 10.83 GiB | 10.83 GiB |
| Gemma 3 27B | Google | HYBRID | 62 | fp8 | 41.62 KiB | 5.20 GiB | 5.20 GiB |
| gpt-oss-20b | OpenAI | HYBRID | 24 | fp8 | 12.01 KiB | 1.50 GiB | 1.50 GiB |

† 131,072 cached tokens exceeds this model's documented or configured maximum context length -- these rows extrapolate the sizing formula past what the model is known to support, not a realizable deployment configuration on their own: Llama 3 8B (max_context=8,192); Qwen3 8B (max_context=40,960); Mistral 7B v0.1 (max_context=8,192); Gemma 2 9B (max_context=8,192).

FP8 storage (1 byte/element) halves every BF16 figure, independent of any model's own weight dtype -- including DeepSeek-V3 and Kimi K2 Instruct, whose public config.json files carry an `fp8` `quantization_config` for their released *weights*. This uniform halving is a simplification specifically for the three MLA rows (DeepSeek-V2, DeepSeek-V3, Kimi K2 Instruct): vLLM's actual production FP8 KV layout for DeepSeek-V3 keeps the decoupled RoPE key component at 2 bytes/element and adds a small per-token scale, giving 656 bytes/token/layer -- 39.1 KiB total, per the "Shrinking the Cache at the Source: Attention Architectures" section above [[61](#ref-61)] -- rather than this tool's simple half of the BF16 figure (34.31 KiB), a roughly 12% undercount. Treat this tool's FP8 column for MLA models as an approximation; the GQA and hybrid rows, where every stored element is uniformly quantized, are not subject to this caveat. This tool's `native_dtype="bf16"` default for those two describes the KV-cache/activation dtype it assumes for sizing, not a claim about the checkpoint's own weight storage format; whether their weight-level FP8 quantization extends to the attention/KV path is not established from config.json alone. gpt-oss-20b's own config makes the general pattern concrete: its `quantization_config` excludes `model.layers.*.self_attn` from mxfp4 conversion, i.e. OpenAI's config documents attention as running outside its low-precision weight format.

At 128K tokens, Mistral 7B v0.1's 4096-token uniform sliding window and gpt-oss-20b's 128-token/1:1 local:global design cap their caches far below any uncapped GQA model of comparable size, at the cost of no layer (Mistral) or half the layers (gpt-oss) retaining exact long-range token-level context -- though of the two, Mistral's design is the more aggressive cache reducer in practice: its all-sliding architecture caps the total at 512 MiB with zero marginal growth past 4096 tokens, versus gpt-oss-20b's 3.00 GiB and continued linear growth from its 12 full-attention layers, despite gpt-oss's smaller raw window number (128 vs 4096). The three MLA models cluster around 8-9 GiB despite layer counts (60-61) well beyond Llama 3 8B's 32, and not far off Llama 3.1 70B's 80, consistent with DeepSeek-V2's own reported "equal to GQA with only 2.25 groups" characterization [[2](#ref-2)]. Gemma 3's smaller 1024-token window and 5:1 local:global ratio (versus Gemma 2's 4096-token window and 1:1 ratio [[64](#ref-64)]) roughly halve its total at this length despite having 20 more layers than Gemma 2 -- the two models happen to have identical bytes/layer/position (8,192 B), since $8 \times 256 = 16 \times 128 = 2{,}048$ cached elements per layer either way, which isolates the window/ratio effect rather than reflecting a general property of the Gemma 2 -> Gemma 3 architecture change. (Google's own ablation of this ratio/window change, on a 2B text-only model at a 32K-token context, shows KV-cache memory overhead falling from ~60% to under 15% with a 1:3 ratio and a 1024-token window [[64](#ref-64)] -- the closest ablated setting to, but not the same configuration as, the 5:1 ratio profiled in the table above; the report does not give a single overhead percentage for the shipped 5:1 architecture at 32K.) Bytes/token above is exact and constant for GQA and MLA rows; for the hybrid rows it is an average over the full 128K sequence, since sliding-window layers stop accumulating once tokens exceed their window while full layers keep growing (`kv_cache_bytes()` also reports the marginal, steady-state bytes/token separately).

Mao, and Ruiyang Zhou. “Energy Characterization of KV Cache Offloading Under Agentic Workloads.” *HotCarbon 2026 (Workshop on Sustainable Computer Systems) / ACM SIGENERGY Energy Informatics Review*, 2026. <https://hotcarbon.org/assets/2026/paper-20.pdf> Verified by direct fetch 2026-09-27 (Georgia Tech authors, per-endpoint production agentic trace replay from Applied Compute on NVIDIA H200s, Llama-3.1-8B, vLLM 0.11.0+). Table 1 and surrounding text confirm: below-90%-p95-KV-utilization conditions show energy deltas within noise of idle-power variance (+-2-3W, 2.0-3.9%); above-90% conditions show 14.4-31.9% GPU energy savings; host CPU/DRAM energy difference is statistically indistinguishable from zero (-0.015+-0.009 J/token, n=15); the data-movement asymmetry is ~0.2 nJ/byte (PCIe move) vs ~100 nJ/byte (GPU recompute), a >=400x gap, ~10^5 FLOPs/byte; throughput-collapse failure mode drops GPU power to 177-182W with output limited to ~27,000 tokens vs 117-158k normally.

<a id="ref-37"></a>37. Tri Dao, Daniel Y. Fu, Stefano Ermon, Atri Rudra, and Christopher Ré. “FlashAttention: Fast and Memory-Efficient Exact Attention with IO-Awareness.” *Advances in Neural Information Processing Systems 35 (NeurIPS 2022)*, 2022. <https://arxiv.org/abs/2205.14135> IO-aware exact attention: reduces HBM accesses from Θ(Nd+N^2) to Θ(N^2 d^2 M^-1) and memory footprint from quadratic to linear in N. Theorem 1 gives the forward pass's FLOP count explicitly as O(N^2 d), the same asymptotic class as standard attention. Separately, the paper's own Figure 2 benchmark (forward+backward combined) reports FlashAttention's total FLOP count as slightly higher than standard attention's (66.6 vs. 75.2 GFLOPs in their GPT-2 test), explicitly attributed to recomputation in the backward pass — a training-specific effect, not applicable to the forward-pass-only prefill/decode path used at inference.

<a id="ref-38"></a>38. Tri Dao. “FlashAttention-2: Faster Attention with Better Parallelism and Work Partitioning.” *The Twelfth International Conference on Learning Representations (ICLR 2024)*, 2024. <https://arxiv.org/abs/2307.08691> Accepted as an ICLR 2024 poster (confirmed via OpenReview's listing and the ICLR 2024 proceedings site; first posted to arXiv in July 2023). Improves GPU occupancy and warp-level work partitioning over FlashAttention; reaches up to 73% of theoretical peak FLOPs/s on A100. Does not change the asymptotic FLOP count of exact attention.

Gulavani, and Ramachandran Ramjee. “Sarathi: Efficient LLM Inference by Piggybacking Decodes with Chunked Prefills.” *arXiv:2308.16369*, 2023. <https://arxiv.org/abs/2308.16369> Introduces chunked-prefills (splitting a prefill request into equal-sized chunks) and decode-maximal batching ('piggybacking' decodes onto a prefill chunk). DistServe [@distserve] cites this paper (together with Sarathi-Serve) as the origin of 'chunked-prefill with piggyback'; reported gains (e.g. up to 10x decode throughput for LLaMA-13B on an A6000 GPU) are model- and hardware-specific.

<a id="ref-41"></a>41. Amey Agrawal, Nitin Kedia, Ashish Panwar, et al. “Taming Throughput-Latency Tradeoff in LLM Inference with Sarathi-Serve.” *18th USENIX Symposium on Operating Systems Design and Implementation (OSDI 2024)*, 2024. <https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.02310> Confirmed via the USENIX OSDI'24 proceedings (usenix.org/system/files/osdi24-agrawal.pdf): published at OSDI 2024, not MLSys 2024 (a related but distinct Agrawal et al. paper, 'Vidur: A Large-Scale Simulation Framework for LLM Inference', was the one accepted at MLSys 2024). Extends Sarathi's chunked-prefill with 'stall-free scheduling', admitting new requests into a running batch via prefill chunks without pausing ongoing decodes; reported gains (2.6x-5.6x serving capacity vs. vLLM) are model- and hardware-specific.

Jiang, Devendra Singh Chaplot, Diego de las Casas, et al. “Mistral 7B.” *arXiv:2310.06825*, 2023. <https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.06825> Table 1 gives dim=4096, n_layers=32, head_dim=128, n_heads=32, n_kv_heads=8, window_size=4096, context_len=8192. Section 2 ('Rolling Buffer Cache') states only one cache-memory figure: 'On a sequence length of 32k tokens, this reduces the cache memory usage by 8x, without impacting the model quality.' There is no stated halving/2x memory figure at sequence length 8192 anywhere in the paper; the paper's only other nearby quantitative figure is an unrelated 2x *speed* improvement from FlashAttention/xFormers changes at a 16K sequence length, not a memory figure and not at 8192 tokens.

<a id="ref-63"></a>63. Gemma Team, and Google DeepMind. “Gemma 2: Improving Open Language Models at a Practical Size.” *arXiv:2408.00118*, 2024. <https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.00118> Table of architecture parameters gives, for the 9B model, 42 layers, 16 attention heads, 8 KV heads, head size 256, sliding window 4096, 1:1 local:global interleave.

<a id="ref-64"></a>64. Gemma Team, and Google DeepMind. “Gemma 3 Technical Report.” *arXiv:2503.19786*, 2025. <https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.19786> States (Sec. 2, main text) that the 5:1 local:global layer ratio with sliding window 1024 is Gemma 3's shipped configuration. Figure 5's ablation, however, is run on a 2B-parameter, text-only model and reports the 60%->15% KV-cache memory overhead reduction at 32k context for a 1:3 local:global ratio with sw=1024, not the shipped 5:1 ratio ('the "global only" configuration results in a memory overhead of 60%, while this is reduced to less than 15% with 1:3 and sliding windows of 1024'). Figure 6 separately plots KV memory vs. context length for the actual 5:1/sw=1024 architecture against a global-only 2B baseline, but does not restate the specific percentage figures for that configuration. Figure 3 shows minimal perplexity impact when varying the local:global ratio from 1:1 up to 7:1, in a separate ablation.

<a id="ref-65"></a>65. OpenAI. “gpt-oss-120b & gpt-oss-20b Model Card.” *OpenAI technical report*, 2025. <https://cdn.openai.com/pdf/419b6906-9da6-406c-a19d-1bb078ac7637/oai_gpt-oss_model_card.pdf> States gpt-oss-120b has 36 layers alternating banded (window 128 tokens) and fully dense attention, 64 query heads of dimension 64, and GQA with 8 KV heads (group size 8).

<a id="ref-66"></a>66. William Brandon, Mayank Mishra, Aniruddha Nrusimha, Rameswar Panda, and Jonathan Ragan-Kelley. “Reducing Transformer Key-Value Cache Size with Cross-Layer Attention.” *Advances in Neural Information Processing Systems 37 (NeurIPS 2024)*, 2024. <https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.12981> CLA2 combined with MQA gives a further 2x KV cache reduction with under 1% perplexity change, in 1B- and 3B-parameter experiments.

<a id="ref-67"></a>67. Yutao Sun, Li Dong, Yi Zhu, et al. “You Only Cache Once: Decoder-Decoder Architectures for Language Models.” *Advances in Neural Information Processing Systems 37 (NeurIPS 2024)*, 2024. <https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.05254> Self-decoder produces one global KV cache reused by all cross-decoder layers; reports ~80x less KV cache memory at 65B scale and 2.87x-71.8x faster prefill from early-exit, up to 1M context.

<a id="ref-68"></a>68. Opher Lieber, Barak Lenz, Hofit Bata, et al. “Jamba: A Hybrid Transformer-Mamba Language Model.” *arXiv:2403.19887*, 2024. <https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.19887> Released 32-layer configuration places attention at every 8th layer (attn_layer_period=8, attn_layer_offset=4), i.e. 4 of 32 layers; paper states this gives an 8x smaller KV cache than a vanilla Transformer.

<a id="ref-69"></a>69. Qwen Team. “Qwen3-Next: Towards Ultimate Training & Inference Efficiency.” *Alibaba Cloud Community*, 2025. <https://www.alibabacloud.com/blog/602580> States Gated DeltaNet and full (Gated) Attention are mixed at a 3:1 ratio (75% Gated DeltaNet layers, 25% full-attention layers).

<a id="ref-70"></a>70. Kimi Team. “Kimi Linear: An Expressive, Efficient Attention Architecture.” *arXiv:2510.26692*, 2025. <https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.26692> Hybridizes Kimi Delta Attention (KDA) with full MLA layers at a uniform 3:1 ratio; reports up to 75% less KV cache usage and up to 6x decoding throughput at 1M-token context versus a matched full-MLA baseline.

<a id="ref-71"></a>71. MiniMax, Aonian Li, Bangwei Gong, et al. “MiniMax-01: Scaling Foundation Models with Lightning Attention.” *arXiv:2501.08313*, 2025. <https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.08313> Places one softmax/GQA layer after every 7 lightning-attention (linear) layers, motivated by lightning attention alone showing 'limited retrieval capabilities' in the authors' own ablations.

<a id="ref-72"></a>72. NVIDIA. “Nemotron-H: A Family of Accurate and Efficient Hybrid Mamba-Transformer Models.” *arXiv:2504.03624*, 2025. <https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.03624> States self-attention layers are kept to ~8% of total layers (4 of 52 in the 8B model, 10 of 118 in the 56B model), using GQA with 8 KV heads. States (Sec. 1): 'With larger sequences (65536 input sequence length, 1024 output tokens), we measure up to 3x higher inference throughput on NVIDIA H100 GPUs' versus similarly-sized Qwen2.5/Llama-3.1 models. Per the companion NVIDIA ADLR project page (research.nvidia.com/labs/adlr/nemotronh/), the 8B model's largest pairwise figure is 3x vs. Llama-3.1-8B (1.8x vs. Qwen2.5-7B), while the 56B model's is 2.4x vs. Qwen2.5-72B/Llama-3.1-70B (2.9x after MiniPuzzle distillation to the 47B variant) -- the abstract's 'up to 3x' is the largest single pairwise comparison across the family, not a uniform multiplier.

As read (issue last updated 2026-06-30), prefix caching for Mamba1 (#26377), ShortConv, LinearAttention and Gated DeltaNet/GDN (#30877, #26807) are all checked off as done; 'Enable align-mode prefix caching by default for hybrid models' remains unchecked. The feature is opt-in via the --mamba-cache-mode flag, not yet the engine default.

The document's separate FlexGen-comparison table covers exactly five models: OPT-30B, OPT-66B, OPT-175B, BLOOM-176B, and LLaMA2-70B (not OPT-6.7B/13B, and not an 'OPT-176B' model, which does not exist -- 176B is BLOOM's size). FlexGen is marked 'Unsupported' for BLOOM-176B and LLaMA2-70B in that table, so only OPT-30B/66B/175B have a direct head-to-head figure; on those three, ZeRO-Inference's best configuration exceeds FlexGen's best configuration (22.74 vs 21.97 on OPT-30B; 8.08 vs 6.24 on OPT-66B; 2.26 vs 1.84 on OPT-175B).

Continuously-updated documentation page; content verified as of September 2026. Cited here in place of the FlexGen paper, which uses Accelerate only as an external throughput baseline and is not itself a source for Accelerate's own offload feature set.

Per the blog's own text, the 7 tok/s Gen4 baseline was measured earlier on an Azure NC_A100_v4 (A100-class) VM, while the 17 and 26 tok/s Gen5 figures were measured on an Azure ND-H200-v5 (H200-class) VM ('We previously evaluated LLAMA-3-70B generation performance ... in an Azure NC_A100_v4 VM ... we repeat the experiments on Azure ND-H200-v5 offering Gen5 NVMes'); the GPU generation therefore changes alongside the NVMe generation between the Gen4 baseline and the Gen5 points, so the full 7->17->26 progression is not a single-variable NVMe-bandwidth comparison, though the 17->26 step alone holds the GPU/VM fixed. A separate SGLang-integration experiment in the same blog uses batch size 128 on a single H200; that figure is not part of this NVMe-scaling result.

<a id="ref-185"></a>185. <a id="ref-187"></a>187. <a id="ref-188"></a>188. Nicole Hemsoth Prickett. “A New Memory Tier for AI at Scale.” *VAST Data blog*, 2026. <https://www.vastdata.com/blog/a-new-memory-tier-for-ai-at-scale> Accessed 2026-09-27. Thought-leadership recap of a VAST Forward 2026 stage conversation. <a id="ref-198"></a>198. Mohammad Shoeybi, Mostofa Patwary, Raul Puri, Patrick LeGresley, Jared Casper, and Bryan Catanzaro. “Megatron-LM: Training Multi-Billion Parameter Language Models Using Model Parallelism.” *arXiv:1909.08053*, 2019. <https://arxiv.org/abs/1909.08053> Introduces column-parallel partitioning of the query/key/value projections so that each attention head's matrix multiply, and hence its K/V state, is computed and held entirely on one GPU.

<a id="ref-199"></a>199. Nidhi Bhatia, Ankit More, Ritika Borkar, et al. “Helix Parallelism: Rethinking Sharding Strategies for Interactive Multi-Million-Token LLM Decoding.” *arXiv:2507.07120*, 2025. <https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.07120> NVIDIA paper. States that when tensor-parallel width exceeds the number of KV heads (as with GQA/MLA), the KV cache is duplicated rather than sharded, capping batch size; introduces Helix Parallelism (KV-parallel attention sharding plus re-provisioned tensor/expert parallelism for the FFN) reporting up to 32x more concurrent users for DeepSeek-R1 and 4x throughput/1.13x interactivity for Llama-405B, simulated on GB200 NVL72 at FP4.

<a id="ref-200"></a>200. NVIDIA. “Context Parallel Overview.” *NVIDIA Megatron-LM GitHub repository documentation (docs/user-guide/features/context_parallel.md)*, n.d. <https://github.com/NVIDIA/Megatron-LM/blob/main/docs/user-guide/features/context_parallel.md> Describes context parallelism (CP) as partitioning the sequence dimension of KV across GPUs, with an all-gather of KV in the forward pass and reduce-scatter of activation gradients in the backward pass, exchanged in a ring topology between GPUs; compatible with GQA/MQA/MHA and combinable with TP, PP and DP. Framed entirely around training (forward/backward propagation, avoiding ~30% full-activation-recomputation overhead); does not mention inference, decoding or serving.

<a id="ref-201"></a>201. NVIDIA. “Helix Parallelism.” *TensorRT-LLM documentation*, n.d. <https://nvidia.github.io/TensorRT-LLM/features/helix.html> States 'Helix is a context parallelism (CP) technique for the decode/generation phase of LLM inference'; describes KV cache partitioning across CP ranks during generation and re-provisioning of CP ranks as tensor-parallel ranks for FFN/MoE layers. Currently supports MLA models (DeepSeek-V3/V3-Lite) on Blackwell GPUs in disaggregated (prefill/decode-split) serving.

Verified present verbatim in this exact version/URL by direct fetch on 2026-09-27.

Breaks down TTFT overhead without RDMA by transport: InfiniBand/RoCE RDMA with GPUDirect +200-500ms (expected), AWS EFA+libfabric+GDRCopy +37ms (measured, AWS p5.48xlarge, Llama-3.1-8B ISL=8000/OSL=50), TCP fallback +90-100s (measured, ~98s TTFT) -- i.e. roughly 200-500x versus RDMA on that reference hardware.

Purely analytical (no new hardware experiments); calibrated to published H100 power measurements, with B200/H200 figures stated as projections at +-20% uncertainty. Abstract confirms: 'tokens per watt halves every time the context window doubles'; H100 16 sequences/64K context (1.5 tok/W) vs. 256 sequences/4K context (17.6 tok/W); two-pool context-length routing (FleetOpt) ~2.5x over a homogeneous fleet; H100->B200 ~1.7x; combined ~4.25x.

<a id="ref-251"></a>251. vLLM Project. “[RFC]: Cache Salting for Secure and Flexible Prefix Caching in vLLM (Issue #16016).” *GitHub, vllm-project/vllm*, 2025. <https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/issues/16016> Submitted by GitHub user dr75. Proposes and compares a 'single-barrier' design (one salt per request, implemented in PR #17045) and a 'multi-barrier' design (per-message salts via a cache_salt_map, isolating tenant suffixes while leaving a shared prefix unsalted). Closed May 2025 with 'Closing now and getting back to multi barrier design (part B) only if needed.' Accessed Sept. 2026, including a 2026-08-17 follow-up comment.

<a id="ref-252"></a>252. vLLM Project. “[Frontend][Core] Add hierarchical cache salting for shared prefixes across tenants (PR #37961).” *GitHub, vllm-project/vllm*, 2026. <https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/37961> Submitted by GitHub user vpfkfl753; references RFC #16016. Source of the '256 tenants -> 256x duplicate prefill of the same system prompt blocks' figure for flat, single-barrier cache_salt. Closed August 2026 as 'effectively unreviewable' after being open five months with an unreviewed 19-file diff; superseded by PR #52655.

<a id="ref-253"></a>253. vLLM Project. “[Core] Support token-range cache salt regions in block hashing (PR #52655).” *GitHub, vllm-project/vllm*, 2026. <https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52655> Submitted by GitHub user vpfkfl753; supersedes PR #37961, splitting out just the core block-hashing contract for RFC #16016's multi-barrier design. Open and unmerged as of September 2026 (last updated 2026-08-21); engine propagation and the chat-API cache_salt_map mapping are implemented separately but not yet opened as their own PRs.

<a id="ref-260"></a>260. Does not contain a client configuration-parameter reference (see mooncake-2026-deployguide / mooncake-2026-configpy). Accessed Sept. 2026.

<a id="ref-267"></a>267. Meta AI (Llama Team). “Meta-Llama-3-8B config.json.” *Hugging Face Hub*, 2024. <https://huggingface.co/meta-llama/Meta-Llama-3-8B/raw/main/config.json> Accessed 2026-09-27. The architecture-relevant fields this tool uses (num_hidden_layers=32, num_key_value_heads=8, hidden_size=4096, num_attention_heads=32 -> head_dim=128) match what is publicly documented for the gated original; the mirror file itself was not independently checked to be byte-for-byte identical to Meta's own copy. max_position_embeddings=8192, rope_scaling=null -- no context extension is configured, so this is the model's documented context length; 128K-token (131,072) sizing in this tool's tables is an extrapolation past it.

<a id="ref-276"></a>276. Gemma Team, Google DeepMind. “gemma-3-27b-it config.json.” *Hugging Face Hub*, 2025. <https://huggingface.co/google/gemma-3-27b-it/raw/main/config.json> Accessed 2026-09-27. <a id="ref-277"></a>277. OpenAI. “gpt-oss-20b config.json.” *Hugging Face Hub*, 2025. <https://huggingface.co/openai/gpt-oss-20b/raw/main/config.json> Accessed 2026-09-27. Public, ungated repo, fetched directly. num_hidden_layers=24, num_key_value_heads=8, head_dim=64 (explicit field), num_attention_heads=64, sliding_window=128; layer_types explicitly alternates sliding_attention/full_attention across all 24 layers. max_position_embeddings=131072, within this tool's 128K comparison. Also carries quantization_config: {quant_method: 'mxfp4', modules_to_not_convert: ['model.layers.*.self_attn', 'model.layers.*.mlp.router', 'model.embed_tokens', 'lm_head']} -- i.e. OpenAI's own config explicitly excludes the attention path from mxfp4 weight quantization, which is the basis for this tool's native_dtype=bf16 assumption for KV cache across all models in this table (see the docstring note on DeepSeek-V3/Kimi-K2, whose configs carry no equivalent exclusion list).
